# Current reviewed progress

The both-tight-mu specialization is accepted after root and independent manual review. Equal tight mu values force every present P column bound to have the same cap Y=a0/max(n0,n1), including equal-coefficient and zero-nu ties. With both caps absent, finite equal demands improve the primary objective. With one missing cap, improvement exists exactly when that column's pure-axis reward is positive; a zero axial reward leaves a neutral ray attached to a strictly negative total score.

The proposed blanket exclusion when both caps are present required correction. With A0=36*p*a0/960, R=14*p*(n0+n1)/960 and r=min(n0,n1)/max(n0,n1), the proposed bound -A0+2RY equals p*a0*(28r-8)/960. It is negative only for r<2/7, which has not been established at the actual law. The exact maximum is the largest of the diagonal score B=-A0+SY and the two bounded axis scores. Both axis scores are strictly negative, so the maximum's sign is exactly B's. The necessary lex-optimal diagonal-price inequality is strict; equality gives an original full-vector lex descent. A failed upper-bound shortcut is not a realizable native counterexample.

**Research and identifiability checkpoint**

The published checkpoint starts from commit
`OBSCURED-ID-01588`. Its fifteen selected source paths
need only existing package initializers, published research consumers and the
standard library at runtime. Pytest is a development test dependency; no local
packaging configuration, core, RET, claims module or measured data is needed.
Independent source and mathematical review found no publication blocker.

Verification in the isolated candidate passed **203 focused pytest cases**
(47 kernel, 48 certificate, 108 identifiability) and **90 legacy research
checks** across nine groups, with no failures or errors. Only eight runner
groups differ from the published runner; the ninth kernel group is already
identical. The coordinator verified the remaining runner functions and
top-level statements against the published version. The full working runner
contains unrelated baseline work and is deliberately not the staged source.
The original working file was preserved while the reviewed snapshot was staged.

[Commit OBSCURED-ID-00704](https://github.com/omekagardens/det_8_framework/commit/OBSCURED-ID-01586)
contains those fifteen source paths and four coordinator Markdown files.
Its tree is `OBSCURED-ID-01589`. Every staged source
was byte-compared with the tested candidate. The normal push to `origin/ret`
succeeded and independent remote-ref lookup returned the full commit above.
The index was empty afterward; the full working runner retained its prior hash.

The research interface includes numerical refusal and tolerance contracts,
not exact floating-point positivity certification or public-core promotion.
The identifiability interface certifies a supplied rational linear mean map
on unrestricted real parameters. It returns either a row-space reconstruction
or an exact nullspace counterexample. It does not provide noisy confidence,
physical feasibility, model selection or measured application benefit.

**RI-11 complete publication checkpoint**

Verified commit `OBSCURED-ID-01587` publishes
the completed scope: six application/script modules, four focused
test files, exactly two changed legacy runner groups and the corrected
application document. The shared validation helper is already published.
Runtime needs only the standard library and published package/helper sources;
legacy `.Z` compatibility additionally invokes the existing system gzip tool.
Pytest is a development dependency. No downloader, IBM ingestion, polling,
core, RET, claims or packaging changes are included.

The original broader ingestion/operational test files and runner remain in
the working tree. Publication uses reviewed clock-only test snapshots and a
HEAD-based runner snapshot; independent review confirmed exact retained
function bodies and unchanged runner bytes outside the two selected groups.
The final isolated candidate passed **105 focused tests** and **33 legacy
checks**, after fixing the integration failure documented in the
[progress record](REVIEW_PROGRESS.md). Every Python file is pinned there.
The application Markdown status was updated after testing; executable bytes
remain the tested versions. This does not constitute measured validation.

**Remaining dependency decisions**

The comparator's observed RET closure uses eleven files under `det8/ret`:
`__init__.py`, `_codec.py`, `_planning.py`, `_score_arithmetic.py`,
`contracts.py`, `covariance.py`, `inference.py`, `provenance.py`, `replay.py`,
`validation_resources.py` and `workflow.py`. It also needs the modified
`relational_tomography.py` helpers `_chi_square_quantile`, `_finite_vector`
and `_mahalanobis_from_cholesky`; the published scheduler is sufficient.
No `det8.core` module was loaded or required in the captured demo closure.
The RET provenance inventory hashes 42 paths, while the captured run loads
18 local sources: 29 hashed paths are not loaded, and two applied helpers plus
three package initializers are loaded without inclusion in that identity.
That inventory is neither the execution closure nor a complete binding of
the applied demo. Publishing every hashed SDK path is not a substitute for
reviewing the actual prerequisites and provenance contract.

The five deferred claims paths are `det8/claims.py`,
`scripts/export_claim_registry.py`, `docs/CLAIM_REGISTRY.md`,
`det8/tests/test_claim_boundaries.py` and `det8/tests/test_claim_summary.py`.
The module's standard-library runtime does not make the package standalone:
it advertises unpublished core support and RET preview surfaces. Its checks
also need current event/legacy/validation boundaries and Track A/gravity
refusals absent from the published source. The generated reference has 35
distinct file targets: 23 are already identical remotely, four are modified
locally and eight are absent remotely at the audit base. Historical G1 source
evidence must not be inherited by later source changes. Preserving the legacy
claim JSON digests means preserving the local accepted baseline; it is not a

**RI-46 independently accepted — an all-size occupation-controlled law**

Root and a separate complete mathematical reviewer accept the analytic
[selection theorem](../track_b/native_growth_clone_selection_v1/SELECTION.md). It keeps the complete RI-41 prefix through parent
size four and explicitly selects every later level within RI-38's conditional
scalar-passive model. For a parent whose maximal vertices have the same strict
past C, the birth at C lies in a private marked-core component with unit loops.
There is exactly one labeled preferred ideal in each such row and none in
other rows. This proves that its large preferred probability is compatible
with locality, equivariance, strict row normalization and every full-D scalar
diamond. Actual earlier probabilities are recomputed in each later potential.

With eta_n=1/(n+1), all other proper births have total mass below eta_n/2.
Preferred births stay suitable and full births restore suitability from any
parent. Thus unsuitable next-parent probability is below eta_n/2 at every
history, and full-birth probability is above eta_n/2. Conditional harmonic
bounds give expected height Theta(log N). Dyadic Markov/Borel-Cantelli and
monotonicity prove H_N/N -> 0 almost surely and in every finite positive
moment. A separate conditional no-full-birth product proves infinitely many
full births, hence H_N -> infinity almost surely. Width also diverges from
N<=height*width. All conditional sums restart at the specified finite cutoff.

These are analytic statements; no executable, layer table, simulator or old
suite was run. Reviews distinguish expected logarithmic height from an
unproved almost-sure logarithmic bound, vanishing occupation from eventual
suitability, and finite positive moments from L-infinity convergence. RI-45's
specified unsuitable Q has drift above 11/12 under this law, so its finite
separator remains valid; this law's worst-row drift tends to one.

The construction is a new chosen conditional law, not DET entailment,
informative quantum dynamics, a manifold, a physical clock or gravity. Its
one-file reservation is released. Only administrative adjudication text is
changed from the stable handoff. RI-48 is assigned after review to determine

conditional exponential-moment proof is welcome. Every full birth increases

pairs, while another proper birth creates at most n. Verify the pathwise bound
I_N<=I_m+N W_N+N B_N, retaining arbitrary initial pairs and exact finite-N
normalization by binom(N,2). Any sharper constants must be justified, not needed.

almost-sure versions force d_N ->1 almost surely. Give a finite conditional

0<rho<1 for every schedule in this mechanism. It does not exclude density one
